&lt;project_context>
# 项目: structfuse-context

## 运行环境
- 服务器名: bjb1-42106
- SSH 账户: root（调用 ssh_execute / submit_training_job 时传入 account="root"）
- SSH 连接命令: ssh connect.bjb1.seetacloud.com  # 必须用此命令，~/.ssh/config 自动应用密钥和跳板机，禁止用裸 IP
- 项目代码根目录: /root/structfuse


## 环境搭建
连接服务器后先调 `detect_image` 检测是否为预置镜像。如果匹配，跳过基础环境搭建，只装项目特有依赖。已预装的包不要重复安装。

## 输出风格
回复简洁直接。只输出执行结果和必要信息，不要解释过程、不要客套、不要给未被要求的建议。
&lt;/project_context>
## 任务：记录 hard_negative_mining.py vs build_m1_training_data.py 实现差异

**背景**：Reviewer 发现 DataFlowAwareMiner（在 hard_negative_mining.py 中）使用 embedding→filter→BM25 三步法选择 negatives，而 pre-mined 训练数据（由 build_m1_training_data.py 生成）使用纯 BM25 排序。当前 M2 训练不受影响（用的是 pre-mined 数据），但 plan_015（dynamic negative mining）实施前必须对齐两者的实现。

**步骤**：
1. SSH 到 bjb1-42106 (root)，项目路径 /root/structfuse
2. 读取 hard_negative_mining.py，找到 DataFlowAwareMiner 类的实现，记录其 negative selection 逻辑（embedding→filter→BM25 三步法的具体代码）
3. 读取 build_m1_training_data.py（或相关的 pre-mine 脚本），记录其 dataflow_aware negative selection 逻辑
4. 对比两者差异，明确：
   - hard_negative_mining.py: 先用 embedding 选 top-K candidates → filter by dataflow → BM25 re-rank
   - build_m1_training_data.py: 直接用 BM25 排序（无 embedding 步骤）
5. 用 save_project_file 将差异记录追加到 memory.md。格式：

```
## hard_negative_mining.py vs build_m1_training_data.py 实现差异 [2026-05-15]

**发现者**：Reviewer
**影响**：当前 M2 不受影响（用 pre-mined），plan_015 实施前必须对齐
**差异**：
- hard_negative_mining.py DataFlowAwareMiner: [具体逻辑]
- build_m1_training_data.py: [具体逻辑]
**对齐建议**：[你的分析]
```

**注意**：先读 memory.md 现有内容，追加新内容，不要覆盖已有内容。

Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). task-agnostic schema but no data-flow semantics |\\n| **LocAgent** | Graph-Guided LLM Agents for Code Localization | ACL 2025 (arXiv:2503.09089) | Directed heterogeneous graph (code entities + dependency edges) | Inference-time | No. Dependency edges, not data-flow | Fault localization | 92.7% file-level accuracy; SWE-bench +12% Pass@10 | Agent multi-hop graph reasoning at inference; needs fine-tuned 32B model |\\n| **RANGER** | RANGER — Repository-Level Agent for Graph-Enhanced Retrieval | arXiv:2509.25257 (Sep 2025) | Knowledge graph + MCTS traversal | Inference-time | No | Code search, QA, cross-file retrieval, completion | CodeSearchNet, RepoQA, RepoBench, CrossCodeEval | MCTS-guided graph exploration; dual-stage (Cypher + MCTS); higher inference cost due to cross-encoder scoring |\\n| **RepoGraph** | Enhancing AI SE with Repository-level Code Graph | ICLR 2025 (arXiv:2410.14684) | Repo-level code graph (class hierarchy, call graph, file structure) | Inference-time | No. Structural relations only | Software engineering / issue resolution | SWE-bench (+32.8% relative); CrossCodeEval | Plug-in module for existing agents; repo-wide navigation; no semantic analysis |\\n| **GraphCoder** | Enhancing Repo-Level Code Completion via Code Context Graph | ASE 2024 (arXiv:2406.07003) | Code Context Graph (CFG + CDG + DDG) | Inference-time | Partial. Data dependence graph but **intra-procedural** only | Code completion | Repository-level benchmarks | Coarse-to-fine retrieval with CCG; closest to StructFuse in using data/control flow, but intra-proc and at inference-time |\\n| **CodeRAG** | Supportive Code Retrieval on Bigraph for Real-World Code Generation | arXiv:2504.10046 (Apr 2025) | Bigraph (requirement graph + DS-code graph) | Inference-time | No. Dependency + semantic edges | Repo-level code generation | DevEval (+40.90 Pass@1 w/ GPT-4o) | Dual-graph (requirements + code); agentic reasoning; high inference overhead |\\n| **ARISE** | ARISE: A Repository-level Graph Representation and Toolset for Agentic Fault Localization and Program Repair | arXiv:2605.03117 (May 2026), Seddik & Fard | Multi-granularity program graph (statement nodes + def-use edges) | Inference-time | Partial. **Intra-procedural** def-use edges + data-flow slicing tool | Fault localization + repair | SWE-bench Lite (300 issues); +17.0 Function Recall@1 | Closest to StructFuse in data-flow usage; but intra-proc only, agent queries at inference |\\n| **CodeGRAG** | Bridging NL and PL via Graphical RAG | arXiv:2405.02355 (2024) | Composed syntax graph (AST + DFG + CFG) | Both (GNN training + graph prompt at inference) | Partial. DFG extraction but function-level | Code generation (cross-lingual) | Multiple PLs | Only method with training-time component (GNN soft prompts); but graph also used at inference |\\n| **StructFuse** (Ours) | Structure-Grounded Hard Negatives via CodeQL Data-Flow | — | Inter-procedural data-flow graph (CodeQL taint tracking) | **Training-time only** | **Yes. Full inter-procedural** (source→sink across call boundaries) | Code retrieval (contrastive training) | Large-scale repos with CodeQL | Zero inference overhead; graph used offline for negative mining; inter-proc data-flow |\\n\\n## Discussion 要点\\n\\n### StructFuse vs Inference-Time Graph Methods: 互补性与差异性\\n\\n1. **Cost-accuracy tradeoff**: Inference-time methods (CodexGraph, RANGER, RepoGraph) achieve high precision by constructing and querying graphs per request, but incur significant latency (graph construction + traversal + LLM calls). StructFuse amortizes all graph computation to training time—once the encoder is trained with structure-grounded negatives, inference is a single embedding lookup with zero graph overhead. This makes StructFuse suitable for latency-sensitive production retrieval where inference-time graph methods are impractical.\\n\\n2. **Data-flow depth**: Most inference-time methods use structural graphs (call graphs, class hierarchies, file dependencies). Only GraphCoder and ARISE incorporate data/control flow, but limited to **intra-procedural** scope. StructFuse uniquely leverages **inter-procedural** data-flow via CodeQL's taint tracking, capturing cross-function semantic relationships (e.g., a sanitizer function that transforms data between source and sink across call boundaries). This deeper semantic signal generates harder, more informative negatives.\\n\\n3. **Complementarity**: StructFuse and inference-time methods are orthogonal and composable. A StructFuse-trained encoder can serve as the embedding backbone for systems like RANGER or CodexGraph, providing better initial retrieval that reduces the graph agent's search space. Conversely, inference-time graph methods can provide reranking on top of StructFuse retrieval for tasks requiring maximum precision.\\n\\n4. **Scalability model**: Inference-time methods scale linearly with query volume (each query triggers graph operations). StructFuse's cost is front-loaded: CodeQL analysis runs once per repository update, and the resulting negatives improve the encoder permanently. For high-QPS code search services, this one-time investment yields compounding returns.\\n\\n5. **Task generality vs specialization**: Inference-time graph agents (CodexGraph, RepoGraph) are task-agnostic but require powerful LLMs as backends. StructFuse produces a specialized retrieval encoder that excels at the code search task without LLM inference, making it deployable as a lightweight, efficient first-stage retriever in multi-stage pipelines.\\n\\n## Related Work 段落草稿 (~200 words)\\n\\n**Graph-Based Methods for Code Understanding.**\\nRecent work has explored leveraging code graphs to enhance LLM-based software engineering. Inference-time approaches construct and query graphs during each interaction: CodexGraph (Liu et al., NAACL 2025) builds a code graph database that LLM agents query via structured interfaces; RepoGraph (Ouyang et al., ICLR 2025) provides repository-level navigation as a plug-in module for SE agents; LocAgent (ACL 2025) and RANGER (Shah et al., 2025) employ graph-guided multi-hop reasoning and MCTS-based traversal for code localization and retrieval, respectively. GraphCoder (ASE 2024) incorporates intra-procedural control-flow and data-dependence graphs for code completion retrieval, while ARISE (Seddik & Fard, 2026) extends this with statement-level def-use edges for fault localization. CodeGRAG (2024) bridges training and inference by using GNN-encoded graph prompts.\\n\\nThese methods achieve strong results but impose substantial inference-time costs—graph construction, traversal, and often multiple LLM calls per query. Moreover, their data-flow analysis remains intra-procedural. StructFuse takes a fundamentally different approach: we leverage CodeQL's inter-procedural taint tracking to generate structure-grounded hard negatives at **training time**, producing an encoder that captures cross-function data-flow semantics without any graph overhead at inference. This positions StructFuse as complementary to inference-time graph methods—it can serve as an efficient first-stage retriever whose embeddings already encode deep structural relationships that other methods must compute on-the-fly.\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 参考文献（全部已验证 2026-05-14）\\n\\n- CodexGraph: arXiv:2408.03910, Liu et al., NAACL 2025\\n- LocAgent: arXiv:2503.09089, Chen et al., ACL 2025\\n- RANGER: arXiv:2509.25257, Shah et al., 2025 ✅ verified\\n- RepoGraph: arXiv:2410.14684, Ouyang et al., ICLR 2025\\n- GraphCoder: arXiv:2406.07003, Liu et al., ASE 2024\\n- CodeRAG: arXiv:2504.10046, 2025\\n- ARISE: arXiv:2605.03117, Seddik & Fard, 2026 ✅ verified (full title: \\\"ARISE: A Repository-level Graph Representation and Toolset for Agentic Fault Localization and Program Repair\\\")\\n- CodeGRAG: arXiv:2405.02355, 2024\\n\\n---\\n\\n## Checkpoint Criterion 与 K 分析 (2026-05-15)\\n\\n**Checkpoint 选择 criterion 分歧率**：4 个完成的 full M2 runs 中，2/4 (50%) val_loss 与 R@1 选中不同 checkpoint。\\n\\n**分歧 pattern**：\\n- Qwen s43: val_loss→step 500, R@1→step 1500, R@1 差 +1.97pp\\n- StarCoder s42: val_loss→step 1000, R@1→step 2500, R@1 差 +0.64pp\\n- Mean delta: +0.65pp (R@1 criterion 优于 val_loss criterion)\\n- StarCoder 步差更大（1500 步 vs 1000 步），与其 pretrained margin 更大的特性一致\\n\\n**论文写作决策**：主表报 best_by_R@1（target metric aligned），ablation 表展示两种 criterion 对比（D036 已决策 R@1 为 primary）。\\n\\n**K=7 无 ablation**：所有正式实验固定 K=7。合理性来源：(1) batch_size=8 对齐（1 positive + 7 negatives = 8）(2) DPR 等文献惯例 (3) hard_negative_ratio=0.5 平衡设计。K 敏感性列为 limitation/future work。\\n\\n---\\n\\n## BM25 Negative Mining 策略洞察 (2026-05-14, updated)\\n\\n**两层重叠率数据（注意区分）**：\\n- **k=50 全候选重叠**（mining worker 报告）：BM25 vs embedding 87.1%, vs random 76.2%, vs dataflow 71.8%\\n- **top-7 Jaccard**（训练实际用的 num_negatives=7，分层分析）：差异远大于全候选\\n\\n**分层 top-7 Jaccard 重叠率**（seed 42, 2920 matched queries / 12290 total）：\\n| 层级 | BM25 vs Embedding | BM25 vs Random | BM25 vs Dataflow |\\n|------|-------------------|----------------|------------------|\\n| Small (<50 files, 39 repos) | 0.668 | 0.411 | 0.667 |\\n| Medium (50-200, 29 repos) | 0.380 | 0.067 | 0.363 |\\n| Large (200+, 15 repos) | 0.297 | 0.007 | 0.405 |\\n\\n**核心修正**：之前基于 87% 预测\\\"Phase 2 中 BM25 R@1 很可能接近 embedding\\\"——这个预测可能不成立。在中大 repo（训练数据主体）上 top-7 Jaccard 仅 30-38%，BM25 和 embedding 选出的训练 negatives 大部分不同。Phase 2 训练结果将真正区分两种策略。\\n\\n**意外发现**：BM25 vs dataflow 在大 repo 上重叠率（0.405）高于 vs embedding（0.297）。词汇相似性信号与数据流依赖有部分对齐，而纯 embedding 距离选择了不同维度的 hard negatives。\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 运维经验 (2026-05-14)\\n\\n**Registry 状态机 workaround**：当长链训练（多 run 串行）中途因 cron timeout 或子进程退出导致 registry 状态误标（DONE/failed 或 ANALYZING），而实际训练仍在跑时，不可回退状态。解决方案：创建 `{exp_id}_resumed` / `{exp_id}_continued` 新 entry，步进 DFG extraction but function-level | Code generation (cross-lingual) | Multiple PLs | Only method with training-time component (GNN soft prompts); but graph also used at inference |\\\\n| **StructFuse** (Ours) | Structure-Grounded Hard Negatives via CodeQL Data-Flow | — | Inter-procedural data-flow graph (CodeQL taint tracking) | **Training-time only** | **Yes. Full inter-procedural** (source→sink across call boundaries) | Code retrieval (contrastive training) | Large-scale repos with CodeQL | Zero inference overhead; graph used offline for negative mining; inter-proc data-flow |\\\\n\\\\n## Discussion 要点\\\\n\\\\n### StructFuse vs Inference-Time Graph Methods: 互补性与差异性\\\\n\\\\n1. **Cost-accuracy tradeoff**: Inference-time methods (CodexGraph, RANGER, RepoGraph) achieve high precision by constructing and querying graphs per request, but incur significant latency (graph construction + traversal + LLM calls). StructFuse amortizes all graph computation to training time—once the encoder is trained with structure-grounded negatives, inference is a single embedding lookup with zero graph overhead. This makes StructFuse suitable for latency-sensitive production retrieval where inference-time graph methods are impractical.\\\\n\\\\n2. **Data-flow depth**: Most inference-time methods use structural graphs (call graphs, class hierarchies, file dependencies). Only GraphCoder and ARISE incorporate data/control flow, but limited to **intra-procedural** scope. StructFuse uniquely leverages **inter-procedural** data-flow via CodeQL's taint tracking, capturing cross-function semantic relationships (e.g., a sanitizer function that transforms data between source and sink across call boundaries). This deeper semantic signal generates harder, more informative negatives.\\\\n\\\\n3. **Complementarity**: StructFuse and inference-time methods are orthogonal and composable. A StructFuse-trained encoder can serve as the embedding backbone for systems like RANGER or CodexGraph, providing better initial retrieval that reduces the graph agent's search space. Conversely, inference-time graph methods can provide reranking on top of StructFuse retrieval for tasks requiring maximum precision.\\\\n\\\\n4. **Scalability model**: Inference-time methods scale linearly with query volume (each query triggers graph operations). StructFuse's cost is front-loaded: CodeQL analysis runs once per repository update, and the resulting negatives improve the encoder permanently. For high-QPS code search services, this one-time investment yields compounding returns.\\\\n\\\\n5. **Task generality vs specialization**: Inference-time graph agents (CodexGraph, RepoGraph) are task-agnostic but require powerful LLMs as backends. StructFuse produces a specialized retrieval encoder that excels at the code search task without LLM inference, making it deployable as a lightweight, efficient first-stage retriever in multi-stage pipelines.\\\\n\\\\n## Related Work 段落草稿 (~200 words)\\\\n\\\\n**Graph-Based Methods for Code Understanding.**\\\\nRecent work has explored leveraging code graphs to enhance LLM-based software engineering. Inference-time approaches construct and query graphs during each interaction: CodexGraph (Liu et al., NAACL 2025) builds a code graph database that LLM agents query via structured interfaces; RepoGraph (Ouyang et al., ICLR 2025) provides repository-level navigation as a plug-in module for SE agents; LocAgent (ACL 2025) and RANGER (Shah et al., 2025) employ graph-guided multi-hop reasoning and MCTS-based traversal for code localization and retrieval, respectively. GraphCoder (ASE 2024) incorporates intra-procedural control-flow and data-dependence graphs for code completion retrieval, while ARISE (Seddik & Fard, 2026) extends this with statement-level def-use edges for fault localization. CodeGRAG (2024) bridges training and inference by using GNN-encoded graph prompts.\\\\n\\\\nThese methods achieve strong results but impose substantial inference-time costs—graph construction, traversal, and often multiple LLM calls per query. Moreover, their data-flow analysis remains intra-procedural. StructFuse takes a fundamentally different approach: we leverage CodeQL's inter-procedural taint tracking to generate structure-grounded hard negatives at **training time**, producing an encoder that captures cross-function data-flow semantics without any graph overhead at inference. This positions StructFuse as complementary to inference-time graph methods—it can serve as an efficient first-stage retriever whose embeddings already encode deep structural relationships that other methods must compute on-the-fly.\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n## 参考文献（全部已验证 2026-05-14）\\\\n\\\\n- CodexGraph: arXiv:2408.03910, Liu et al., NAACL 2025\\\\n- LocAgent: arXiv:2503.09089, Chen et al., ACL 2025\\\\n- RANGER: arXiv:2509.25257, Shah et al., 2025 ✅ verified\\\\n- RepoGraph: arXiv:2410.14684, Ouyang et al., ICLR 2025\\\\n- GraphCoder: arXiv:2406.07003, Liu et al., ASE 2024\\\\n- CodeRAG: Program Repair\\\\\\\")\\\\n- CodeGRAG: arXiv:2405.02355, 2024\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n## Checkpoint Criterion 与 K 分析 (2026-05-15)\\\\n\\\\n**Checkpoint 选择 criterion 分歧率**：4 个完成的 full M2 runs 中，2/4 aligned），ablation 表展示两种 criterion 对比（D036 已决策 R@1 为 primary）。\\\\n\\\\n**K=7 无 ablation**：所有正式实验固定 K=7。合理性来源：(1) batch_size=8 对齐（1 positive + 7 negatives = 8）(2) DPR 等文献惯例 (3) hard_negative_ratio=0.5 平衡设计。K 敏感性列为 limitation/future work。\\\\n\\\\n---\\\\n\\\\n## BM25 Negative Mining 策略洞察 (2026-05-14, updated)\\\\n\\\\n**两层重叠率数据（注意区分）**：\\\\n- **k=50 全候选重叠**（mining worker 报告）：BM25 vs embedding 2 训练结果将真正区分两种策略。\\n\\n**意外发现**：BM25 vs dataflow 在大 repo 上重叠率（0.405）高于 vs embedding（0.297）。词汇相似性信号与数据流依赖有部分对齐，而纯 embedding 距离选择了不同维度的 hard negatives。\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 运维经验 (2026-05-14)\\n\\n**Registry 状态机 workaround**：当长链训练（多 run 串行）中途因 cron timeout 或子进程退出导致 registry 状态误标（DONE/failed 或 ANALYZING），而实际训练仍在跑时，不可回退状态。解决方案：创建 `{exp_id}_resumed` / `{exp_id}_continued` 新 entry，步进 PREPARING→QUEUED→ALLOCATED→RUNNING，注册 cron 监控实际 PID。原 entry 保留历史 metrics。\\n\\n**Bash 预读陷阱**：运行中的 bash 脚本已将循环体/数组预读到内存，磁盘上修改脚本文件不影响执行。需要在链外设置独立 tmux watcher 监控链 PID 退出后手动触发后续任务。\\n\\n**BM25 Phase 2 触发机制**：两个 tmux watcher（`bm25_starcoder_watcher`, `bm25_qwen_watcher`）每 5 分钟检查链 PID 存活，退出后自动启动 BM25 脚本。watcher 启动的训练不经过 submit_training_job，需在链完成回调中补注册 cron。\\n\\n---\\n\\n## 关键决策记录\\n\\n**m1_reextract_v2 数据使用决策 (2026-05-14, Director Session 8)**：即使 reextract 将数据从 ~16K 增至 ~44K，不重跑 M2。原因：ARR May 25 DDL 不允许再加 4-5 天重训。处理方式：(1) post-M2 完成后若有 GPU 空档，跑 1-seed pilot (Qwen, embedding, 新数据) 对比当前 M2 baseline；(2) 论文中作为 scalability/data efficiency 讨论点；(3) 全量重训留 camera-ready。\", \"total_chars\": 10859, \"truncated\": false}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {}}"
```json
    }
```
```json
  },
```

```text
{"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"\\\"\\\"\\\"Data-flow-aware hard negative mining for StructFuse.\\n\\nThree strategies:\\n  1. random         — baseline, random non-positive from same repo\\n  2. embedding_distance — CoCoHaNeRe-style nearest-neighbour non-positives\\n  3. dataflow_aware — lexically similar but data-flow disconnected (our contribution)\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nfrom __future__ import annotations\\n\\nimport random as stdlib_random\\nfrom abc import ABC, abstractmethod\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\nfrom typing import Dict, List, Set, Tuple\\n\\nimport numpy as np\\n\\n\\nclass HardNegativeMiner(ABC):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Base class for hard-negative mining strategies.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    def __init__(\\n        self,\\n        dataflow_graph: Dict[str, Set[str]] | None = None,\\n        embeddings: Dict[str, np.ndarray] | None = None,\\n        bm25_index: object | None = None,\\n    ):\\n        self.dataflow_graph = dataflow_graph or {}\\n        self.embeddings = embeddings or {}\\n        self.bm25_index = bm25_index\\n\\n    @abstractmethod\\n    def mine(\\n        self,\\n        query: str,\\n        positives: List[str],\\n        candidates: List[str],\\n        num_negatives: int,\\n    ) -> List[Tuple[str, float]]:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Return list of (candidate_id, hardness_score) tuples.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        ...\\n\\n    # ── shared helpers ──────────────────────────────────────────────\\n\\n    @staticmethod\\n    def _exclude(candidates: List[str], exclude: Set[str]) -> List[str]:\\n        return [c for c in candidates if c not in exclude]\\n\\n    @staticmethod\\n    def load_embeddings(path: str | Path) -> Dict[str, np.ndarray]:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Load pre-computed embeddings from a .npz file (keys=file ids).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        data = np.load(path, allow_pickle=True)\\n        return {str(k): data[k] for k in data.files}\\n\\n    @staticmethod\\n    def save_embeddings(embs: Dict[str, np.ndarray], path: str | Path) -> None:\\n        np.savez(path, **embs)\\n\\n\\nclass RandomMiner(HardNegativeMiner):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Uniformly random negatives from same-repo non-positives.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    def mine(self, query, positives, candidates, num_negatives):\\n        pool = self._exclude(candidates, {query, *positives})\\n        sampled = stdlib_random.sample(pool, min(num_negatives, len(pool)))\\n        return [(s, 0.0) for s in sampled]\\n\\n\\nclass EmbeddingDistanceMiner(HardNegativeMiner):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Nearest-neighbour negatives by cosine similarity (CoCoHaNeRe baseline).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    def __init__(self, **kwargs):\\n        super().__init__(**kwargs)\\n        self._matrix: np.ndarray | None = None\\n        self._keys: List[str] | None = None\\n\\n    def _build_matrix(self):\\n        if self._matrix is not None:\\n            return\\n        self._keys = sorted(self.embeddings.keys())\\n        vecs = np.stack([self.embeddings[k] for k in self._keys]).astype(np.float32)\\n        norms = np.linalg.norm(vecs, axis=1, keepdims=True).clip(min=1e-8)\\n        self._matrix = vecs / norms\\n\\n    def mine(self, query, positives, candidates, num_negatives):\\n        self._build_matrix()\\n        if query not in self.embeddings:\\n            return RandomMiner().mine(query, positives, candidates, num_negatives)\\n\\n        exclude = {query, *positives}\\n        q_vec = self.embeddings[query].astype(np.float32)\\n        q_vec = q_vec / max(np.linalg.norm(q_vec), 1e-8)\\n\\n        # cosine similarity against all candidates\\n        cand_set = set(self._exclude(candidates, exclude))\\n        indices = [i for i, k in enumerate(self._keys) if k in cand_set]\\n        if not indices:\\n            return []\\n\\n        sims = self._matrix[indices] @ q_vec  # (N_cand,)\\n        top_k = min(num_negatives, len(indices))\\n        top_idx = np.argpartition(-sims, top_k)[:top_k]\\n        top_idx = top_idx[np.argsort(-sims[top_idx])]\\n\\n        return [(self._keys[indices[i]], float(sims[i])) for i in top_idx]\\n\\n\\nclass DataFlowAwareMiner(HardNegativeMiner):\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"Select lexically/embedding-similar but data-flow-disconnected files.\\n\\n    Core insight: a file that *looks* related (high BM25 or embedding sim) but\\n    has no data-flow path to the query provides the strongest contrastive signal.\\n    \\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\n    def __init__(self, top_k_similar: int = 50, **kwargs):\\n        super().__init__(**kwargs)\\n        self.top_k_similar = top_k_similar\\n        self._emb_miner = EmbeddingDistanceMiner(\\n            embeddings=self.embeddings,\\n            dataflow_graph=self.dataflow_graph,\\n        )\\n\\n    def _is_reachable(self, src: str, dst: str) -> bool:\\n        return dst in self.dataflow_graph.get(src, set())\\n\\n    def mine(self, query, positives, candidates, num_negatives):\\n        exclude = {query, *positives}\\n\\n        # Step 1: get top-K similar candidates (by embedding distance)\\n        similar = self._emb_miner.mine(\\n            query, positives, candidates, self.top_k_similar\\n        )\\n\\n        # Step 2: filter to those NOT reachable via data-flow\\n        disconnected = [\\n            (fid, score)\\n            for fid, score in similar\\n            if fid not in exclude and not self._is_reachable(query, fid)\\n        ]\\n\\n        # Step 3: if BM25 index available, re-rank by BM25 similarity\\n        if self.bm25_index is not None and hasattr(self.bm25_index, \\\"get_scores\\\"):\\n            disconnected = self._rerank_bm25(query, disconnected)\\n\\n        selected = disconnected[:num_negatives]\\n\\n        # fallback: pad with random if not enough disconnected candidates\\n        if len(selected) < num_negatives:\\n            used = {fid for fid, _ in selected} | exclude\\n            pool = [c for c in candidates if c not in used]\\n            extra = stdlib_random.sample(pool, min(num_negatives - len(selected), len(pool)))\\n            selected.extend([(e, 0.0) for e in extra])\\n\\n        return selected\\n\\n    def _rerank_bm25(\\n        self, query: str, candidates: List[Tuple[str, float]]\\n    ) -> List[Tuple[str, float]]:\\n        \\\"\\\"\\\"Re-rank disconnected candidates by BM25 score (descending).\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n        from rank_bm25 import BM25Okapi\\n\\n        if not isinstance(self.bm25_index, BM25Okapi):\\n            return candidates\\n\\n        # BM25 scores expect a tokenized query — use simple whitespace split\\n        scores = self.bm25_index.get_scores(query.split())\\n        id_to_bm25: Dict[str, float] = {}\\n        if hasattr(self.bm25_index, \\\"_doc_ids\\\"):\\n            for idx, doc_id in enumerate(self.bm25_index._doc_ids):\\n                id_to_bm25[doc_id] = float(scores[idx])\\n\\n        reranked = sorted(\\n            candidates,\\n            key=lambda t: id_to_bm25.get(t[0], 0.0),\\n            reverse=True,\\n        )\\n        return {"result":"{\"success\": true, \"data\": {\"stdout\": \"#!/usr/bin/env python3\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"Build contrastive training data from M1 full extraction results.\\n\\nReads individual M1 extraction JSON files, reads source code from repo\\ndirectories, and produces per-strategy JSONL files compatible with\\ntraining/train.py.\\n\\nKey design: two levels of connectivity:\\n  - positive_files: direct inter-file dataflow connections (training positives)\\n  - full_connected: positive_files + call graph neighbors (broader, for\\n    dataflow_aware negative filtering)\\n\\nThis ensures embedding and dataflow_aware strategies produce DIFFERENT\\nnegative pools: embedding includes call-graph-connected files as negatives,\\nwhile dataflow_aware excludes them.\\n\\\"\\\"\\\"\\n\\nimport argparse\\nimport json\\nimport os\\nimport random\\nfrom collections import defaultdict\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\nimport time\\n\\nrandom.seed(42)\\n\\nMAX_FILE_CHARS = 4096\\nCANDIDATE_POOL_SIZE = 50\\nMAX_PER_REPO = 200\\n\\n\\ndef collect_py_files(repo_path: str) -> dict[str, str]:\\n    files = {}\\n    for root, dirs, filenames in os.walk(repo_path):\\n        dirs[:] = [d for d in dirs Dataflow graph (for train.py's hard negative mining)\\n            gid = iid + \\\"::\\\" + buggy_file\\n            dataflow_graph[gid] = [iid + \\\"::\\\" + cf for cf in full_connected]\\n            for cf in full_connected:\\n                cid = iid + \\\"::\\\" + cf\\n                if cid not in dataflow_graph:\\n                    dataflow_graph[cid] = []\\n                dataflow_graph[cid].append(gid)\\n\\n            # Non-positives: exclude only positive_files (NOT full_connected)\\n            non_pos = [f for f in all_py if f != buggy_file and f not in positive_files]\\n\\n            if args.embedding_mining_mode == \\\"static\\\":\\n                bm25 = compute_bm25_scores(source_files[buggy_file], all_py, source_files)\\n                if miner is not None and repo_embeddings is not None:\\n                    emb_scores = miner.compute_scores(buggy_file, all_py, repo_embeddings)\\n                else:\\n                    emb_scores = bm25\\n                pools = build_candidate_pools(non_pos, emb_scores, bm25, full_connected, args.pool_size)\\n            else:\\n                pools = {}\\n\\n            if args.embedding_mining_mode == \\\"static\\\":\\n                if set(pools[\\\"embedding\\\"]) != set(pools[\\\"dataflow\\\"]):\\n                    stats[\\\"total_pos_diff\\\"] += 1\\n                stats[\\\"total_instances\\\"] += 1\\n\\n            repo_data[iid] = {\\n                \\\"buggy_file\\\": buggy_file,\\n                \\\"source_files\\\": source_files,\\n                \\\"valid_pos\\\": list(positive_files),\\n                \\\"pools\\\": pools,\\n                \\\"full_connected\\\": full_connected,\\n            }\\n\\n        print(f\\\"  {n_with_pos} instances with positives, {len(all_py)} .py files\\\")\\n\\n    print(f\\\"\\\\nStrategy divergence: {stats['total_pos_diff']}/{stats['total_instances']} \\\"\\n          f\\\"({100*stats['total_pos_diff']/max(stats['total_instances'],1):.1f}%) instances have different embedding vs dataflow pools\\\")\\n\\n    # Build forward + reverse instances\\n    base_instances = []\\n    for iid, rd in repo_data.items():\\n        for pos_file in rd[\\\"valid_pos\\\"]:\\n            base_instances.append({\\\"iid\\\": iid, \\\"query_file\\\": rd[\\\"buggy_file\\\"], \\\"pos_file\\\": pos_file})\\n            base_instances.append({\\\"iid\\\": iid, \\\"query_file\\\": pos_file, \\\"pos_file\\\": rd[\\\"buggy_file\\\"]})\\n\\n    print(f\\\"Total base instances (fwd+rev): {len(base_instances)}\\\")\\n\\n    # ````text
```
````text
Now I have a complete picture. Let me also check the key logic around line 315-320, specifically how `emb_scores` is computed when `miner is None`:

```python
if args.embedding_mining_mode == "static":
    bm25 = compute_bm25_scores(source_files[buggy_file], all_py, source_files)
    if miner is not None and repo_embeddings is not None:
        emb_scores = miner.compute_scores(buggy_file, all_py, repo_embeddings)
    else:
        emb_scores = bm25  # <-- FALLBACK: embedding scores = BM25 scores
    pools = build_candidate_pools(non_pos, emb_scores, bm25, full_connected, args.pool_size)
```

So when the miner isn't available, embedding scores default to BM25 scores. Then in `build_candidate_pools`, the embedding pool ranks all non-positives by these scores and takes the top-K, while the dataflow pool filters out the fully connected set and ranks by BM25 separately.

Now looking at how `DataFlowAwareMiner` actually works in `hard_negative_mining.py`: it first gets the top-50 candidates by embedding distance, then filters those down to only ones not reachable via dataflow (checking just direct 1-hop connections), and finally re-ranks the remaining candidates by BM25. The key difference from the other approach is that it applies the dataflow filter after embedding ranking rather than building separate pools upfront. The key insight is that without an explicit model path, both strategies collapse to using BM25 as their ranking signal—the real distinction then becomes which candidates they consider in the first place: embedding similarity versus dataflow connectivity.

Now I'm documenting these findings and storing them for reference.
````

SWE-bench +12% Pass@10 | Agent multi-hop graph reasoning at inference; needs fine-tuned 32B model |\n| **RANGER** | RANGER — Repository-Level Agent for Graph-Enhanced Retrieval | arXiv:2509.25257 (Sep 2025) | Knowledge graph + MCTS traversal | Inference-time | No | Code search, QA, cross-file retrieval, completion | CodeSearchNet, RepoQA, RepoBench, CrossCodeEval | MCTS-guided graph exploration; CrossCodeEval | Plug-in module for existing agents; repo-wide navigation; no semantic analysis |\n| **GraphCoder** | Enhancing Repo-Level Code Completion via Code Context Graph | ASE 2024 (arXiv:2406.07003) | Code Context Graph (CFG + CDG + DDG) | Inference-time | Partial. Data dependence graph but **intra-procedural** only | Code completion | Repository-level benchmarks | Coarse-to-fine retrieval with CCG; closest to StructFuse in using data/control flow, but intra-proc and at inference-time |\n| **CodeRAG** | Supportive Code Retrieval on Bigraph for Real-World Code Generation | arXiv:2504.10046 (Apr 2025) | Bigraph (requirement graph + DS-code graph) | Inference-time | No. Dependency + semantic edges | Repo-level code generation | DevEval (+40.90 Pass@1 w/ GPT-4o) | Dual-graph (requirements + code); agentic reasoning; high inference overhead |\n| **ARISE** | ARISE: A Repository-level Graph Representation and Toolset for Agentic Fault Localization and Program Repair | arXiv:2605.03117 (May 2026), Seddik & Fard | Multi-granularity program graph (statement nodes + def-use edges) | Inference-time | Partial. **Intra-procedural** def-use edges + data-flow slicing tool | Fault localization + repair | SWE-bench Lite (300 issues); +17.0 Function Recall@1 | Closest to StructFuse in data-flow usage; but intra-proc only, agent queries at inference |\n| **CodeGRAG** | Bridging NL and PL via Graphical RAG | arXiv:2405.02355 (2024) | Composed syntax graph (AST + DFG + CFG) | Both (GNN training + graph prompt at inference) | Partial. DFG extraction but function-level | Code generation (cross-lingual) | Multiple PLs | Only method with training-time component (GNN soft prompts); but graph also used at inference |\n| **StructFuse** (Ours) | Structure-Grounded Hard Negatives via CodeQL Data-Flow | — | Inter-procedural data-flow graph (CodeQL taint tracking) | **Training-time only** | **Yes. Full inter-procedural** (source→sink across call boundaries) | Code retrieval (contrastive training) | Large-scale repos with CodeQL | Zero inference overhead; graph used offline for negative mining; inter-proc data-flow |\n\n## Discussion 要点\n\n### StructFuse vs Inference-Time Graph Methods: 互补性与差异性\n\n1. **Cost-accuracy tradeoff**: Inference-time methods (CodexGraph, RANGER, RepoGraph) achieve high precision by constructing and querying graphs per request, but incur significant latency (graph construction + traversal + LLM calls). StructFuse amortizes all graph computation to training time—once the encoder is trained with structure-grounded negatives, inference is a single embedding lookup with zero graph overhead. This makes StructFuse suitable for latency-sensitive production retrieval where inference-time graph methods are impractical.\n\n2. **Data-flow depth**: Most inference-time methods use structural graphs (call graphs, class hierarchies, file dependencies). Only GraphCoder and ARISE incorporate data/control flow, but limited to **intra-procedural** scope. StructFuse uniquely leverages **inter-procedural** data-flow via CodeQL's taint tracking, capturing cross-function semantic relationships (e.g., a sanitizer function that transforms data between source and sink across call boundaries). This deeper semantic signal generates harder, more informative negatives.\n\n3. **Complementarity**: StructFuse and inference-time methods are orthogonal and composable. A StructFuse-trained encoder can serve as the embedding backbone for systems like RANGER or CodexGraph, providing better initial retrieval that reduces the graph agent's search space. Conversely, inference-time graph methods can provide reranking on top of StructFuse retrieval for tasks requiring maximum precision.\n\n4. **Scalability model**: Inference-time methods scale linearly with query volume (each query triggers graph operations). StructFuse's cost is front-loaded: CodeQL analysis runs once per repository update, and the resulting negatives improve the encoder permanently. For high-QPS code search services, this one-time investment yields compounding returns.\n\n5. **Task generality vs specialization**: Inference-time graph agents (CodexGraph, RepoGraph) are task-agnostic but require powerful LLMs as backends. StructFuse produces a specialized retrieval encoder that excels at the code search task without LLM inference, making it deployable as a lightweight, efficient first-stage retriever in multi-stage pipelines.\n\n## Related Work 段落草稿 (~200 words)\n\n**Graph-Based Methods for Code Understanding.**\nRecent work has explored leveraging code graphs to enhance LLM-based software engineering. Inference-time approaches construct and query graphs during each interaction: CodexGraph (Liu et al., NAACL 2025) builds a code graph database that LLM agents query via structured interfaces; RepoGraph (Ouyang et al., ICLR 2025) provides repository-level navigation as a plug-in module for SE agents; LocAgent (ACL 2025) and RANGER (Shah et al., 2025) employ graph-guided multi-hop reasoning and MCTS-based traversal for code localization and retrieval, respectively. GraphCoder (ASE 2024) incorporates intra-procedural control-flow and data-dependence graphs for code completion retrieval, while ARISE (Seddik & Fard, 2026) extends this with statement-level def-use edges for fault localization. CodeGRAG (2024) bridges training and inference by using GNN-encoded graph prompts.\n\nThese methods achieve strong results but impose substantial inference-time costs—graph construction, traversal, and often multiple LLM calls per query. Moreover, their data-flow analysis remains intra-procedural. StructFuse takes a fundamentally different approach: we leverage CodeQL's inter-procedural taint tracking to generate structure-grounded hard negatives at **training time**, producing an encoder that captures cross-function data-flow semantics without any graph overhead at inference. This positions StructFuse as complementary to inference-time graph methods—it can serve as an efficient first-stage retriever whose embeddings already encode deep structural relationships that other methods must compute on-the-fly.\n\n---\n\n## 参考文献（全部已验证 2026-05-14）\n\n- CodexGraph: arXiv:2408.03910, Liu et al., NAACL 2025\n- LocAgent: arXiv:2503.09089, Chen et al., ACL 2025\n- RANGER: arXiv:2509.25257, Shah et al., 2025 ✅ verified\n- RepoGraph: arXiv:2410.14684, Ouyang et al., ICLR 2025\n- GraphCoder: arXiv:2406.07003, Liu et al., ASE 2024\n- CodeRAG: arXiv:2504.10046, 2025\n- ARISE: arXiv:2605.03117, Seddik & Fard, 2026 ✅ verified (full title: \"ARISE: A Repository-level Graph Representation and Toolset for Agentic Fault Localization and Program Repair\")\n- CodeGRAG: arXiv:2405.02355, 2024\n\n---\n\n## Checkpoint Criterion 与 K 分析 (2026-05-15)\n\n**Checkpoint 选择 criterion 分歧率**：4 个完成的 full M2 runs 中，2/4 (50%) val_loss 与 R@1 选中不同 checkpoint。\n\n**分歧 pattern**：\n- Qwen s43: val_loss→step 500, R@1→step 1500, R@1 差 +1.97pp\n- StarCoder s42: val_loss→step 1000, R@1→step 2500, R@1 差 +0.64pp\n- Mean delta: +0.65pp (R@1 criterion 优于 val_loss criterion)\n- StarCoder 步差更大（1500 步 vs 1000 步），与其 pretrained margin 更大的特性一致\n\n**论文写作决策**：主表报 best_by_R@1（target metric aligned），ablation 表展示两种 criterion 对比（D036 已决策 R@1 为 primary）。\n\n**K=7 无 ablation**：所有正式实验固定 K=7。合理性来源：(1) batch_size=8 对齐（1 positive + 7 negatives = 8）(2) DPR 等文献惯例 (3) hard_negative_ratio=0.5 平衡设计。K 敏感性列为 limitation/future work。\n\n---\n\n## BM25 Negative Mining 策略洞察 (2026-05-14, updated)\n\n**两层重叠率数据（注意区分）**：\n- **k=50 全候选重叠**（mining worker 报告）：BM25 vs embedding 87.1%, vs random 76.2%, vs dataflow 71.8%\n- **top-7 Jaccard**（训练实际用的 num_negatives=7，分层分析）：差异远大于全候选\n\n**分层 top-7 Jaccard 重叠率**（seed 42, 2920 matched queries / 12290 total）：\n| 层级 | BM25 vs Embedding | BM25 vs Random | BM25 vs Dataflow |\n|------|-------------------|----------------|------------------|\n| Small (<50 files, 39 repos) | 0.668 | 0.411 | 0.667 |\n| Medium (50-200, 29 repos) | 0.380 | 0.067 | 0.363 |\n| Large (200+, 15 repos) | 0.297 | 0.007 | 0.405 |\n\n**核心修正**：之前基于 87% 预测\"Phase 2 中 BM25 R@1 很可能接近 embedding\"——这个预测可能不成立。在中大 repo（训练数据主体）上 top-7 Jaccard 仅 30-38%，BM25 和 embedding 选出的训练 negatives 大部分不同。Phase 2 训练结果将真正区分两种策略。\n\n**意外发现**：BM25 vs dataflow 在大 repo 上重叠率（0.405）高于 vs embedding（0.297）。词汇相似性信号与数据流依赖有部分对齐，而纯 embedding 距离选择了不同维度的 hard negatives。\n\n---\n\n## hard_negative_mining.py vs build_m1_training_data.py 实现差异 [2026-05-15]\n\n**发现者**：Reviewer\n**影响**：当前 M2 不受影响（用 pre-mined 数据），plan_015（dynamic negative mining）实施前必须对齐\n\n**差异**：\n\n### hard_negative_mining.py `DataFlowAwareMiner`（training/hard_negative_mining.py）\n三步法：\n1. **Embedding top-K**：调用 `EmbeddingDistanceMiner.mine()` 获取 top-50 cosine similarity 最高的 candidates（从所有 non-positive candidates 中）\n2. **Dataflow filter**：过滤掉 `_is_reachable(query, fid)` 为 True 的文件——仅检查 `dataflow_graph[src]` 中的直接条目（1-hop 可达性）\n3. **BM25 re-rank**：如果 `bm25_index` 可用，对剩余 disconnected candidates 按 BM25 分数降序重排\n4. **Fallback**：如果 disconnected 不足 `num_negatives`，用 random padding 补足\n\n### build_m1_training_data.py `build_candidate_pools()`（scripts/build_m1_training_data.py）\n两步法：\n1. **Dataflow filter**：从 `non_pos_files` 中排除 `full_connected_set`（= `positive_files ∪ cg_connected`，即 dataflow 直连 + call graph 邻居——比 `DataFlowAwareMiner` 排除范围更广）\n2. **BM25 sort**：对 disconnected 文件按 BM25 分数降序排序，取 top-K（pool_size=50）\n3. **无 embedding 步骤**：dataflow pool 完全不使用 embedding 信号\n\n额外注意：当 `--model-path` 未提供时，`emb_scores = bm25`（line 317-318），此时 embedding pool 也退化为 BM25 排序，仅在是否排除 `full_connected` 上与 dataflow pool 不同。\n\n### 差异总结\n\n| 维度 | hard_negative_mining.py | build_m1_training_data.py |\n|------|------------------------|--------------------------|\n| Candidate 初筛 | Embedding cosine top-50 | 全部 non-positive（无初筛） |\n| Dataflow 排除范围 | `dataflow_graph[src]` 直接条目 | `positive_files ∪ cg_connected`（更广） |\n| 排序信号 | Embedding 为主 → BM25 re-rank | 纯 BM25 排序 |\n| Embedding 角色 | 主选择器（决定候选池） | 不参与 dataflow pool |\n| BM25 角色 | 可选的 re-ranker | 唯一排序信号 |\n\n### 对齐建议\nplan_015 实施 dynamic negative mining 时需统一为一种逻辑。建议：\n1. **排除范围统一**：采用 `build_m1_training_data.py` 的 `full_connected`（dataflow + call graph），因为 call graph 邻居也不应作为 hard negatives\n2. **选择信号**：dynamic mining 应使用当前 epoch 的 encoder embedding（不是 pretrained），这是 dynamic mining 的核心价值——所以 `DataFlowAwareMiner` 的 embedding→filter→BM25 三步法更合理，但需将排除范围从 1-hop dataflow 扩展到 full_connected\n3. **具体改动**：修改 `DataFlowAwareMiner._is_reachable()` 使其也排除 call graph 连接，或在 `mine()` 中接受 `full_connected_set` 参数\n\n---\n\n## 运维经验 (2026-05-14)\n\n**Registry 状态机 workaround**：当长链训练（多 run 串行）中途因 cron timeout 或子进程退出导致 registry 状态误标（DONE/failed 或 ANALYZING），而实际训练仍在跑时，不可回退状态。解决方案：创建 `{exp_id}_resumed` / `{exp_id}_continued` 新 entry，步进 PREPARING→QUEUED→ALLOCATED→RUNNING，注册 cron 监控实际 PID。原 entry 保留历史 metrics。\n\n**Bash 预读陷阱**：运行中的 bash 脚本已将循环体/数组预读到内存，磁盘上修改脚本文件不影响执行。需要在链外设置独立 tmux watcher 监控链 PID 退出后手动触发后续任务。\n\n**BM25 Phase 2 触发机制**：两个 tmux watcher（`bm25_starcoder_watcher`, `bm25_qwen_watcher`）每 5 分钟检查链 PID 存活，退出后自动启动 BM25 脚本。watcher 启动的训练不经过 submit_training_job，需在链完成回调中补注册 cron。\n\n---\n\n## 关键决策记录\n\n**m1_reextract_v2 数据使用决策 (2026-05-14, Director Session 8)**：即使 reextract 将数据从 ~16K 增至 ~44K，不重跑 M2。原因：ARR May 25 DDL 不允许再加 4-5 天重训。处理方式：(1) post-M2 完成后若有 GPU 空档，跑 1-seed pilot (Qwen, embedding, 新数据) 对比当前 M2 baseline；(2) 论文中作为 scalability/data efficiency 讨论点；(3) 全量重训留 camera-ready。"
```json
}
```
